## Indulgent

Indulgents were priests of the Church of Sharess who specialized in spying, seduction, and celebration.

Activities
Indulgents went about their duties to the extreme.

Culture
Though similar in their purpose to Sune's silkwhispers, indulgents performed their duties with a hint of a dark side influenced by Sharess. They did not shy from violence and cruelty when needed. Indulgents were extremely attractive men and women.

Abilities
Indulgents were quite skilled at prying secrets from anyone whom they spent a little bit of time with in a private setting. They could use their wiles to essentially charm a target for twelve hours. These abilities usually only worked on members of the indulgent's race.

They could not turn undead.

Possessions
They favored lace or satin garments usually decorated with patterns of red, white or black. A rich crimson cloak completed their ensemble. Their holy symbol was usually worn as an anklet. They never wore armor.

Indulgents preferred smaller weapons such as daggers, darts, or garottes. They often carried debilitating non-lethal poisons as well.

References

## Ingrar Welven

Ingrar Welven was a boy pirate who was a loyal crew-member of the Kissing Shark helmed by Blackfingers Ralingor in the Utter East in the late 14 century DR and went on to become the new Chosen of Tyr.

Personality
Ingrar was hungry for fortune and adventure. He was usually calm. Ingrar was proud of his country.

Relationships
Ingrar was a kind of mascot for the other Sharkers. He was good friend of Nargin Olnblade, who taught him many lessons.

History
Sometime in the, the Kissing Shark was destroyed by a blast of smoke powder. Blackfingers Ralingor perished and only seven of the Sharkers survived; Ingrar was one of them. Together the Sharkers saved one chest off the Kissing Shark. Later, the group found themselves in the tavern of Donder's Dancing Masques in Tharkar. There, a mysterious benefactor named Belmer Droon rescued them from one of the frequent bar fights, and helped the Sharkers avoid the brutal law enforcement of the Daggers of Tharkar.

Once the Sharkers and their savior reached the safety of the Ankle Bells tavern, Belmer revealed his agenda: for generous pay, he wanted to hire a group of mercenaries to perform a mission in the capital of Doegan. After the pirates agreed, Belmer had them sign writs and they embarked on a mission to find and assassinate a noblewoman, Eidola of Neverwinter. Sharessa Stagwood explained to Ingrar the meaning of the contacts that Belmer had them sign.

Under the cover of night, in secrecy, the group boarded the ship Morning Bird. Soon after the ship left the port, it came under an attack from an unknown black ship that bombarded the Bird with fire pot missiles and terrifying bone balls that melted away in the flames, revealing dancing floating armed skeletons. The Sharkers and their benefactors managed to fight off the undead and evaded the black ship with the aid of Belmer's box of mists and a prayer-token of Umberlee. The box transported the ship into a different part of the sea away from the pursuit, but the prayer token of the fickle goddess summoned her servants on board a ghost ship. The ship spawned the dreaded sea zombies and the crew of Morning Bird had to appease Umberlee by tossing their valuables overboard.

During the short respite, the suspicious employer mixed an exotic drug called yulchass powder in with the crew's food. The powder had a side effect of making its imbibers truthful. The ship followed them, somehow tracking them. After a long search, the Sharkers and  Belmer found a magical scrying device hidden on the bottom on the Sharker's chest.

Soon after, the mysterious black ship, now identified as the Black Dragon, caught up with the Morning Bird. The pursuing captain was the infamous Orim Redbeard, the pirate responsible for the death of Blackfingers Ralingor. Kurthe Lornar's short temper and rabid devotion to his old captain pushed him to attempt to attack Redbeard. But Belmer, expecting this, hit the tall Konigheimer over the head, knocking him out, despite Ingrar expecting their employer reveal some sort of magical weapon of doom. Orim Redbeard realized that he pursued the wrong ship, and left the Sharkers in peace. Meanwhile, Kurthe Lornar came back to his senses, fuming with rage over what he perceived as betrayal. He attacked the benefactor in blind rage, while his fellow Sharkers stayed out of combat. Belmer showed uncanny skill and finesse in avoiding Kurthe's brutal attacks. Giving the hotheaded pirates plenty of warnings, Belmer continued leaving cuts and slices on the Konigheimer's body. The pirate never stood down and died soaked in his own blood with Belmer's dagger in his neck.

Ingrar survived the sinking of the Morning Bird alongside the Sharkers a and a big group of Jander Turbalt's sailors. Surviving the dangers of open waters, they soon encountered the creatures that made Doegan infamously deadly—the "fiends". Many sailors died during the fighting in Doegan's forest. Ingrar believed that the fiends couldn't leave the woods but he was wrong—a bone devil targeted the boy pirate and a branch hit Ingrar's head, ripping out his eyes and maiming his face. Sharessa, Jolloth Burbuck, and one of the sailors bandaged the boy and soon after Ingrar fell asleep. He was carried by Jolloth during the march. The bone devil attacked the Sharkers again and tried to kill Ingrar again but was at last killed by Sharessa.

After arriving in Eldrinpar in Doegan, Ingrar, despite being blinded, fully recovered. In the city the mercenaries led by Artemis Entreri were confronted by the paladins from Waterdeep led by Miltiades. One group was looking to rescue Eidola, the abducted bride of Piergeiron the Paladinson, and the other group to assassinate her. So they started to fight in front of Eldrinpar's Fountain of the Kraken and in battle Ingrar fought as well as he could until his fellow pirate Jolloth "Anvil" Burbuck was killed. After the fight ended, Kastonoph Nesher joined the mercenaries.

Later, during the fiends' invasion of the city, Noph and the mercenaries led by Enteri decided to enter the dungeons of King Aetheric III's palace. There Ingrar sensed the bloodforge and Entreri and Sharessa both insisted on recovering the artifact, before the fiends could seize it.

There the mercenaries lost Noph but met the paladins again. During the ensuing fight, they saw at a distance Eidola stopping the skirmish. Then Enteri ordered Belgin Dree and Nargin to follow her, helped by paladins Miltiades and Jacob, while Ingrar, Entreri and Shar together with paladins Trandon, Kern Desanea went in search of Noph. Ingrar asked Enteri why he was chosen for this particular mission and the assassin said he needed the boy's power to sense the bloodforge.

Using his new power, Ingrar tried to figure where Eidola had taken Noph inside the dungeon. So they found Kastonoph Nesher but the boy pirate sensed a secret in  Trandon.

Afterward, thanks to Ingrar, they found the bloodforge at the same moment cultists from the Fallen Temple were stealing it. However, they were defeated by the cultists and so Trandon finally unveiled himself as one of the War Wizards of Cormyr. After they discussed their next move, Ingrar said that his destiny was linked to the artifact and he needed to recover it.

Soon after, Ingrar sensed that the Mar chancellor of Doegan, Ikavi Garkim, was following them with his guards and they stopped to speak with him. Offering a temporary alliance, the politician and aide of the Emperor joined them and they entered the Fallen Temple to stop the devious plot of the cultists to summon their deity Ysdar and Entreri and Sharessa attempted to claim the bloodforge as their own but were stopped by now-monstrous Aetheric III. In the process, the deity Tyr revealed himself through his new Chosen—Ingrar Welven. The deity manifested himself through ominous red skies and collapsed part of the temple's dome. Disgusted by the cult that claimed to be his worshipers, Tyr incinerated many and forced the survivors to flee. Before disappearing, Tyr tasked Ingrar, proclaimed to be the Voice of Tyr, with rebuilding his church in the Utter East. After the mad Mage-Emperor was also defeated, Garkim and Ingrar took control of the bloodforge in order to defeat the fiends and save the city.

Description
Ingrar was made blind by the fiends. After using his other senses, he gained some kind of power of sensing beneath the surface of things even sensing danger around him.

## Inquest

The Inquest was the a group of brilliant inventors that acted as the governing body of the Abeiran city of Xorvintroth.

Activities
Using the Supreme Forge within Xorvintroth, members of the Inquest crafted a number of powerful and technologically-advanced artifacts. The devices they created greatly improved the lives of citizens within their city.

They accomplished this feat by combining power they harnessed from the Far Realm along with that of psionics.In the adventure Expedition to the Supreme Forge the term "mind magic" is used, rather than psionics.

They recorded their discoveries upon stone tablets, or within in a series of 'memory pillars', that projected illusory schematics upon nearby walls.

Possessions
Notable among the group's inventions were far realm batteries, a 'teleportation ray', used to quickly convey individuals across their city, the autonomous metallic peacekeepers, and perhaps their greatest creation, the dragonbone golem.

Members
The Inquest comprised Xorvintroth's most elite dragonborn artificers. Among these great minds were selected four elders, who actually constructed the group's devices within the Astral Plane.

Notable Members
At the time of the Inquest's ruination, its four elders included Ukthet, Lirith, Krektesh, and Graoul.

Relationships
They maintained a working relationship with the Key Project team, via that group's liaison Ra'Bindranax.

History
Over time, the Inquest's elders became concerned their advancements would be taken over by the dragon rulers of Abeir. They were visited shortly thereafter by a being known as the Emissary of the Nine Eyes, who offered them information about another world on which they could peacefully conduct their research.

The Inquest began construction on a "bridge", or portal, that would pierce the Far Realm, and grant them access to their new ideal world. During their experimentations, an elderly humanoid figure stepped through the portal to the Inquest's side on Xorvintroth. This being was an aspect of the elder evil Father Llymic. He provided the dragonborn scholars invaluable information about the Far Realm, allowing them to finally construct the Vast Gate. Thus was formed a permanent link between the Abeiran city and the Far Realm.

Unfortunately for the Inquest and all the dragonborn of Xorvintroth, the Vast Gate proved to be the harbinger of their doom. The moment it was activated, the fabric of reality was torn asunder and countless horrors from the Far Realm descended upon the city. The Inquest's four elders remained as "spectral echoes" within the Astral Plane, while their still-living brains remained interred within coffins in the ruins of Xorvintroth.

## Inshula sar Mashewe

Inshula sar Mashewe and her sister Kwesi sar Mashewe were huntswomen and bounty hunters active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Inshula was a tall and beautiful Tashlutan woman, just like her sister. However, despite her beauty, her steely gaze and muscular body betrayed her vocation and strength. Some compared her body language to that of a strung bow, ready to shoot an arrow at any moment.

Possessions
Inshula sar Mashewe. She also carried a deck of cards which she consulted when seeking guidance from her deity – Savras. He wore a set of unique enchanted hides and Preyhunter crossbow.

Relationships
Inshula spent most of her life traveling and hunting alongside her sister Kwesi. Her animal companion was a pitch-black fierce panther named Areghaeb.

History
Inshula and Kwesi sar Mashewe were huntresses in training from the city of Tashluta. The women honed their skills in tracking dangerous beasts. In the, the sisters traveled by sea to the hidden nation of Samarach and organized hunting expeditions from the city of Samargol. Their goal was to become stronger and more experienced to travel back home to Chult across the dangerous mountains that separated the two regions of the Chultan Peninsula. During one of their hunts, the sisters came across a legendary giant dire boar Bal'alak. Inshula and Kwesi showered the beast with bolts but could not slay it. Retreating, Kwesi was injured and was forced to make a living dispensing bounties for monster parts in Samargol at the behest of the city's guards as she healed. Inshula, however, was still willing to hunt beasts and offered her services to traveling adventurers. Unlike Kwesi, Inshula was impatient and did not want to deal with Samargol's governors, thinking them cowards.

Eventually, Inshula sar Mashewe traveled to the Sword Coast and spent some time hunting there. However, with time she developed a longing for home and subsequently reunited with Kwesi, and they both returned to Tashluta.

## Incense of somnolence

Incense of somnolence was a type of sleep-inducing incense from the southern land of wizards–Halruaa.The Magehound gives no name to the incense. This page uses a descriptive name.

Description
Incense of somnolence was a non-magical drug-like incense made from a blend of powerful herbs. It could affect even creatures normally resistant or immune to magic, both arcane and divine. Together with consumed herbal infusions, incense of somnolence could keep a creature asleep as long as the dosage and exposure were regulated. In smaller dosages, or when a creature was weaned off its effects, the incense still had an effect, making the targets disorientated and confused.

Notable Owners
Kiva, a Halruaan inquisitrix who used incense of somnolence and herbal infusions to keep a magic-resistant jordaini Andris asleep for four days in the .

## Infrainvisible

Infrainvisible was a spell used by clerics of Shevarash.

Effects
When cast, this spell hides the infrared signature of a targeted creature. This allowed the targeted creature to become invisible to infravision. The spell did not render the targeted creature truly invisible, so they could still be detected by mundane, such as hearing or small, or magical means. Anything carried by the targeted creature also became invisible to infravision, but became visible once released. While the spell was in effect, any item picked up became invisible to infravision. The spell was unable to mask the heat signature of very hot items or fire itself.

The spell lasted up to a full day if not dispelled. The effects of the spell were broken if the targeted creature attacks another.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Shevarash and a single drop of ichor from a beetle.

## Infernal war machine

Infernal war machines were terrible vehicles of war manufactured and utilized by the devils of the Nine Hells, powered by the souls of mortals.

Description
Form

Infernal war machines were constructed of infernal iron and adorned with massive spikes, bladed weapons, and in some cases, massive siege weaponry. They were highly customizable, and in some cases constructed entirely from cobbled-together components of different war machines. Just like more mundane mechanical vehicles, they could be repaired by a skilled mechanic, over the course of some hours.

Their inner machinery included a furnace, that processed damned souls as fuel, and an engine that translated that fuel into mechanical energy. The machinery was unpredictable and even hazardous, especially when pushed to their limits. Engines were prone to excessive smoking or flaring, and it was not unheard of for a furnace to rupture while in operation.

Function
Infernal war machines were constructed to fulfill various roles in battle, specifically for baatezu and other devils to gain advantages against demons of the Abyss in the Blood War. Smaller war machines were built to be used on scouting missions or to transport raiding parties, while others were built as mobile siege platforms or for the primary purpose of mowing down hordes of demonic minions.

Armor, Gadgetry, & Weapons
War machines could be made nigh-indestructible with armor crafted from iron mined in Cania, gilded with gold acquired from the archdevil Mammon, or adorned with spikes inscribed with profane symbiology, which trapped the souls of any creature they impaled.

Some were even equipped with magical smoke screen devices that utilized necrotic energy, or teleporters that could transport the entire vehicle and everyone aboard upwards of  once per day.

They were often outfitted with terrible weapons of war imagined by the most devious of the Hells' fiendish inventors. Notable among these were:
 Acidic bile sprayer, a hose-and-nozzle weapon that showered enemies in the corrosive bile of some unknown creature(s).
 Flamethrower, the weapon station that spewed forth jets of flame that ignited any flammable foes or material.
 Harpoon fingers, cannons that fired massive harpoons towards targets upwards of  away.
 Infernal screamer, a humanoid-shaped figure of wax constructed with a hand crank that inflicted terrible telepathic pain upon anyone nearby.
 Styx sprayer, another form of weaponry that showered foes in caustic liquids, utilizing water pumped out of the River Styx.

Operations
An infernal war machine was controlled by its helm, a typical vehicle seat surrounded by a series of pedals, levers, and steering mechanism. A war machine without a driver would remain on its current course and speed until stopped by some other force.

The engine of an infernal war machine each possessed a receptacle in which soul coins could be inserted. The soul trapped within the coin was immediately siphoned into the machine's furnace, processed into power, and destroyed within a matter of days. Liquid demon ichor could be applied directly into the furnace to bolster performance. While operational, the engine of the war machine resonated with the sonorous rumble, underscored with screams of agony.

Despite their unique power source and quasi-magical nature, infernal war machines were incapable of acting directly on their own and required a crew to be piloted, and its weaponry to be manned. Excessive wear and tear on their machinery could lead to outright failure. Similarly, war machines could be immediately shut down by use of antimagic fields or other magic-nullifying effects.

History
After components of the Infernal Machine of Lum the Mad were recovered, the archdevils Bel and Zariel both had their minions attempt to convert the artifact into an exceptionally powerful infernal war machine.

Types of Infernal War Machines
 Devil's Ride, a small single-rider machine built atop two wheels. It was capable of high speeds and was exceptionally maneuverable.
 Earth Ripper, a huge war machine that could transport larger crews and was equipped with an enormous wrecking ball on a chain.
 Tormentor, the four-wheeled vehicle that could rapidly transport small teams and carry a moderate amount of cargo.
 Demon Grinder, the massive armored vehicles of the infernal armies constructed to slay as many demons as possible in the quickest time possible.
 Scavenger, a bulky war machine built with a massive clawed arm used to haul damaged machines or other debris on the battlefield.

Notable Infernal War Machines Machines
 Soul Reaper, a gargantuan war machine commissioned by Mad Maggie to harvest souls at a terrifying rate.

Notable Owners
 Bazelstreen, the horned devil mechanic of the Stygian dock.
 Fai Chen, a planewalking merchant that found herself heavily indebted to Mahadi, the rakshasa proprietor of the Wandering Emporium.
 Mad Maggie, the night hag warlord and head of the Knucklebones Gang of Avernus.
 Raggadragga, leader of the Goreguts Gang also on Avernus.
 Smiler the Defiler, a fallen eladrin that was forced to survive in the Nine Hells after being abandoned by his comrades in the Feywild.

## Insect ward

Insect ward was a very rare spell used by druids.

Effects
When cast on a weapon, the spell created an invisible barrier up to  in height. The barrier extended between a pair of rune-covered stakes of wood placed by the caster into the ground during casting. The maximum distance between each stake was . This barrier turned away almost every single insect. Any insect trapped within the barrier became lethargic and slowly perished. The caster was capable of using this spell to only target specific insects. This was done by carving unique runes onto the wooden stakes. The spell had no effect on giant-sized insects or insects that were under the magical control of another. The spell was broken when one of the stakes was removed from the ground.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, the pair of rune-covered wooden stakes.

Interdiction was a special divine abjuration quest spell from the law and wards spheres that negatively affected all enemies within the area of effect.

Enemies within a cube measuring  on all sides found it more difficult to resist the effects of magic, more difficult to strike and harm their targets, and found themselves easier to be hit in combat. Allies of the caster experienced the exact opposite effects. Additional effects were possible through interdiction depending on the god who granted the spell. Negative and positive effects would stop affecting an enemy or ally several minutes after they left the area of effect.

This spell was used by druids who served Moander during the height of ancient Netheril's power.

## Inquisitive

Inquisitives were rogues that specialized in discovering truths in the world, by uncovering secrets and solving puzzles of the unknown.

Culture
These individuals were highly valued among the leadership of the Waterdhavian adventuring company Acquisitions Incorporated.

Abilities
These rogues finely honed their ability to process the words and actions of others. They excelled at deductive reasoning, had masterful attention to detail, and let no clue go unexamined. Inquisitives learned to readily identify any lie they heard in conversation.

They specialized in fighting off creatures that preyed upon the innocent and obscured their presence among everyday folk. They trained themselves to see through illusion and obfuscation and to strike true against hidden enemies such as shapechangers.

Their senses became so finely tuned, inquisitives could discern an opponent's weaknesses in battle and strike against where they were most vulnerable.

## Infernal contract

An infernal contract was a binding agreement between one individual and a devil of the Nine Hells. It typically involved the devil granting a significant boon or gift to an individual, and the contractee owing a specific debt to the fiend.

Description
Contracts varied in form and appearance, but were often presented with grand theatricality, such as appearing within a cloud of smoke or delivered by an imp. Some of the more common forms contracts could take included: written in blood on a scroll of vellum, inscribed upon stone formed by rapidly cooling lava, or etched into the flesh of the person in contract with the devil.

While infernal contracts were always written in the Infernal script, they could be endorsed by several means. A devil might only require the bound party to sign upon a parchment while others might dictate an act of depravity be performed. In some instances, contractors required witness by designated infernal contract notaries.

Specifications
Once the devil and the agreeing party each entered into the contract willfully, the devil's boon was bestowed and its beneficiary was indebted to pay a pre-specified price.

If the contractee was unwilling or became otherwise unable to fulfill their end of the bargain, they suffered one or more penalties as outlined by the contract. Customary penalties included the forfeiture of all owned material wealth, the unwilling adoption of one or more fiendish physical characteristics, or transference of one's mortal soul to the offended party.

Ending Contracts

Infernal contracts could be voided so long as both parties agreed. Under these circumstances, the devil and the contractee each continued on with their existences as if the contract never existed to begin with. Devils often requested significant compensation before agreeing to nullify an infernal contract. Compensation for nullification often included endowment of great personal wealth, one or more soul coins, or extremely powerful artifacts.

Contracts that were not fully ratified could be broken by other supernatural means, though the exact specifications of this were unclear. A devil talisman crafted from the blood of an empyrean and powered by the soul of a mortal was one known method.

Notable Infernal Contracts
 The agreement made by the arcanaloth Fetchtatter to the archdevil Bel.
 The contract formed between High Overseer Thavius Kreeg and Archduke Zariel to save Elturel from destruction in 1442 DR.
 The debt accrued by Fai Chen to the rakshasa Mahadi of the Wandering Emporium.
 The agreement made between Krydle of Baldur's Gate and the erinyes Oreasha to save Shandie Freefoot.

## Intercession

Intercession was an enchanted, intelligent longsword that granted the wielder added skill at deflecting incoming attacks.

Description
While the sturdy steel sword appeared rather mundane―with a simple blade, guard, and grip made of ogre-skin leather―it possessed significant power. Intercession possessed slight empathetic intelligence and was capable of guiding the movements of its wielder.

Powers
Intercession struck more true and inflicted more harm than non-magical blades, in thanks to its +1 enchantment. By means of the empathic bond it formed, the sword helped its wielder block and deflect incoming melee attacks.

History
The sword was crafted by Peth, a wizard that lived in Elturel as of the . Peth made the weapon specifically for an acquaintance of his, a ranger from Soubar named Kelhanion.

Kelhanion was killed by over a dozen trolls when traveling through the Trollbark Forest some years later.

Sometime over the next decade, Intercession made its way north to the icy keep of Dragon's Eye in the Spine of the World mountains.

## Incarnum

Incarnum was a form of raw magic, the ever-present magical energy that permeated all life and existence.

Description
Incarnum manifested as wisps of pure magical energy.

Properties
Like other magical practices, such as the Art, the Power, or truename magic, incarnum was one of the numerous means to draw upon the power of the Weave. It was however radically different than those practices.

Availability
Incarnum predominantly appeared in the Realms following profound events that altered or reshaped the Weave. The three deaths of the goddesses of magic were such notable events:
 Mystryl sacrificing her own life, following the events of Karsus' Folly.
 The death of Mystra by Helm during the Time of Troubles.
 The murder of Midnight by Cyric and Shar, at the onset of the Blue Breath of Change.

Additionally, the presence of the gods as mortals during the Time of Troubles was itself significant enough to reshape the Weave and manifest incarnum.

Applications
Wielders of incarnum were known as meldshapers, but had other names depending on how they used their powers. Many incarnates wielded their powers with some ignorance as to their source of origin. They included:
 Incarnates, who embodied extreme principals in their practice of incarnum magic.
 Soulborn, warriors of supernatural power that possessed strong moral tendencies towards good or evil.
 Totemists, tribal shamans who viewed incarnum as a form of old spiritual energy.

In addition to standing within divine presence, incarnates could manifest their powers after being exposed to wild magic or powerful spells or magical phenomena.

History
The amount of accessible incarnum increased throughout the Realms drastically following the, and the Time of Troubles. As the deities of Toril walked upon the world as mortals, they attracted many mortal followers who absorbed the incarnum that manifested in their wake. Some of these followers developed the powers of meldshapers and focused on one extreme aspect of their deity's moral and ethical outlook.

Rumors & Legends
Some scholars that specialized in the study of magic theorized that meldshaping was a primordial form of heavy magic manipulation.

## In Volo's Wake

In Volo's Wake is an Adventurers League module set in the Forgotten Realms using the Dungeons & Dragons 5 edition ruleset. It is an introductory adventure for Volo's Guide to Monsters, and was designed by Monica Valentinelli, Shawn Merwin, and Rich Lescouflair. It focuses on events in the vicinity of Phandalin, and is designed for 1st–to 4 level characters.

Synopsis
In Volo's Wake consists of six "mini-adventures", each of which is intended to take 1-2 hours and highlight one or more creatures from the Volo's Guide to Monsters sourcebook:
The Green Skin of Treachery The party seeks to rescue a noble named Eric Merryweather from blights and goblins in the Crooked Forest.
The Mottled Fur of Rage Sildar Hallwinter asks the party to rescue his adopted daughter from gnolls in the Crooked Forest.
The Filthy Claws of Sin The party travels to Neverwinter Wood to find a missing elf named Delsy, and they encounter a trio of hags.
The Cold Scales of Death The party travels toward Old Owl Well to find an eccentric man known as Master Aumaro, who seeks their help to defeat a group of yuan-ti.
The Tentacled Maw of Evil The party assists a mind flayer named Cerali to discover what has caused its elder brain to act strangely.
The Many Eyes of Doom A mysterious beholder known as Gavmogon has been behind the strife encountered by the party in the preceding adventures, and so they travel to its sanctum to end the threat.

## Infection (adventure)

The Infection is an adventure module set in Malatra – Living Jungle using the Dungeons & Dragons 3 edition ruleset. It is part of the Living Jungle series of adventures published for the RPGA Network. It is an adventure designed for characters of high level – 7-10.

Summary
In The Year of Tigers Curse (1374 DR), after the heroic death of Big Chief Bagoomba, witch-doctor Bengoukee sent a group of heroes of the Living Jungle to a shu village of the Jengi tribe. The shu became stricken with a strange hunger disease; the tribe needed immediate aid. With help from Blu and Buster, the adventurers met the "Great Spirit" who knew how to concoct a cure. In reality, the "Spirit" was one of a group of jungle doppelganger who wanted to cure the shu for their own reasons. The heroes traveled to the Yaku Plains in search of a special luminescent moss for the cure while being trailed and watched by the doppelgängers in their jungle creatures' shape. There, they met a baboon jungle spirit named Rafiki who left the heroes with veiled foreshadowing of the Living Jungle's future and guidance to retrieve the moss. Subsequently, the moss was made into a cure that saved the lives of the Jengi tribe.

## Insect repellent

Insect repellent, also known as parasite repellent, was a class of substances designed to protect the user from the bites of insects and parasites.

Description
Both natural and alchemical or otherwise magical insect repellents existed, and they came in many forms with somewhat different use cases.

Incense
Insect-repelling incense could be used to ward off all normal bugs within a radius of . When lit, a block of such incense would burn for up to eight hours. Such incense sold for 1 silver piece per block as of the late 15 century DR.

Liquids
Alchemists were said to manufacture a strong smelling liquid that was applied to the skin. It worked to repel normal and smaller insects for up to four hours and could even dissuade giant insects or insects under the influence of spells from attacking the wearer. Such substances were sold for 5 gold pieces per dose as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

It was said that certain magical fluids could be sprayed to prevent any insect from approaching the user. Containers of such fluids could sell for 1,000 gold pieces as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Powders
Powdered insect repellents were said to be an alchemical substance that warded off any insects in much the same way that similar magical substances might ward off lycanthropes or undead, although the effect usually only lasted up to 20 minutes. These were produced from ingredients derived from natural flowers including daisies and jasmine. Such powders sold for 200 gold pieces as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Salves
Insect repelling salves were applied to the skin and generally protected only against normal insects. They tended to be ineffective against giant insects or against angry swarms, but in some cases they could be used to diffuse entanglements with larger insects or to help prevent the contraction of diseases from them.

Nonmagical salves tended to be greasy as a result of being water resistant, and could be either odorless or quite pungent. Such salves were sold in vials, jars, or gourds containing enough for between 3 and 20 days of protection, and sold for 1 gold piece per 20 doses as of the late 15 century DR.

A magical version of insect repelling salve was said to be crafted by druids and made from animal fat, herbs, and swamp gas. Such compounds were highly effective at deterring all blood-drinking parasites for as much as 200 minutes, although they were somewhat water soluble: while they were effective at repelling things like leeches and lampreys while in or underwater, the substance washed off after only 60 minutes. These salves also smelled truly horrible, so they were not very popular as the only way to quickly wash them away was with a large quantity of wine. These magical salves sold for as much as 1,000 gold pieces as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Other
Angelicas could serve as a mild insect repellent. The juice of magasoriums and the oil derived from bison-gourds could be utilized as effective insect repellents.

Garlic juice was said to be a natural insect repellent, although its strong smell made it easy to tell when a user of it was coming. Garlic could also be used to soothe venomous insect bites.

Chultan dwarves were known to cover themselves in grease to ward off insects.

In the Othrys layer of Carceri, stinkweed was commonly rubbed on bodies to ward off insects.

The Azuposi people of Maztica used the black cohosh plant as a natural "bugbane".

There also existed a repel insects spell that achieved a similar effect as repellent substances.

History
Folk in the Moonsea region made use of an insect repellent that came in the form of a pungent yellow goop.

As of the late 15 century DR, explorers in Chult were advised to use insect repellent to avoid contracting shivering sickness. The market for insect repellent was strictly regulated in Port Nyanzaru—the only city in Chult at the time—with the sale and production of such repellents being controlled by the merchant prince Kwayothé. Both salves and incense could be bought at the city's Red Bazaar.

## Infernal Death

The Infernal Death was a disease outbreak that struck the Shoon Imperium in the 4 century DR.

Effects
Afflicted individuals experienced mania and mild fevers. There was also a noticeable heightening of the color in infected individuals' eyes, hair, and skin, which gave the false impression that they were healthy. Death could then occur suddenly.

The most terrifying symptom was that once a victim died, their corpse would combust and could start fires.

History
The plague struck during the summer of the, across Calimshan and Tethyr, notably in Calimport, Keltar, Memnon, Myratma, Shoonach, and Teshburl. As much as 9% of the population in these areas died, although the death toll was only 4% in Calimport.

As the plague-filled summer continued, the accumulation of combusting bodies led to the Plague Fires in Calimport.

Appendix
References

## Insect sight

Insect sight was a very rare wizard's spell that granted a creature insect-like all-around eyesight for a limited time.

Effects
When the spell was cast, the wizard touched a creature, granting them a sight similar to that of an insect, with an almost full 360 degrees of field of view. This allowed the ensorcelled creature not to be surprised by a sneak attack.

Insect sight required 12 seconds to cast, and its effect lasted for 30 seconds at its weakest, and the spell's duration grew along with the spell-caster's magical prowess.

Tunnel vision was insect sight's reverse spell, and it reduced a creature's field of vision to 10 degrees. It granted them protection against gaze-based magical attacks and abilities but made them vulnerable to stealing and surprise attacks.

Components
The spell required verbal, somatic, and material components to be cast. Its material components were sunblossom oil and the dried remains of ten flies.

To cast tunnel vision, the caster needed to use nightflower sap and an earthworm.

History
In the 14 century DR, insect sight was recorded in Prismal's Pocket Library, Vol.IV. Prismal the Outrageous himself knew the spell, and he kept a record of it in his magical library at Chemcheaux 223, Ravens Bluff, until the shop's destruction in the late 14 century DR. At some point, one of Prismal's spellbook that contained insect sight left Toril. The book's original name was If They Can Do It, Prismal Can Do It Two; it was filled with Prismal's hand-written notes. It ended up in the hands of several spell dealers on the world of Nehwon where the city of Lankhmar was located.

## Inuksuk

Inuksuk was a technique for hunting caribou employed by the Angulutiun and the Iulutiun of the Great Glacier.

Description
Upon discovering a grazing herd, the hunting party quietly constructed an inuksuk. An inuksuk was a series of snowmen standing at approximately  in height and spaced roughly  apart. Once assembled, the inuksuk took on the form of a V-shape, with each arm of the V extending approximately .

The majority of the hunters positioned themselves at the convergence point of the V, while a select few stealthily maneuvered to the open end of the formation. With everyone in their designated spots, the hunters positioned at the open end unleashed piercing shrieks and screams, propelling the caribou towards the converging point of the V. There, they are greeted with a relentless barrage of arrows.

Usage
The inuksuk technique for hunting caribou was originally developed by the Angulutiuns, although they seldom employed it themselves. However, it was frequently used by the Iulutiuns.

## Inixrien

Inixrien, the Tenth City of the Sword, was one of the twelve major cities in the imperial heartlands of ancient Jhaamdath during the Founding Time.

Geography
The Tenth City was far east of the capital Naarkolyth on the south side of the Bay of Jhaamdath. After its destruction, the ruins lay in the Selmal Basin in the Vilhon Reach.

History
Inixrien was founded in, as the tenth of the realm's Cities of the Sword.

By, the empire's long-running struggle with Unther led to the Untheric conquest of the Akanal region and Jhaamdath being invaded directly. Inixrien was attacked, and despite the defense by the heroic Hlath the city was taken, with much of eastern Jhaamdath taken by . This conquest was held until, when Jhaamdath's counter-conquest reclaimed much of what had been lost.

In the year of, Jhamdaath began a campaign of regional expansion that led to a brutal war with the elves of Nikerymath. In response, the  saw the elves use a High Magic ritual to destroy the heartlands of Jhaamdath with a great wave from the Inner Sea. The massive movement of water created the Vilhon Reach and sank Inixrien like the rest of the realm, and its psionic artifacts were buried in the sea floor where their emanations affected the nearby sealife.

Sunken Jhaamdath was later revealed by the dropping of sea levels after the Spellplague in 1385 DR, becoming the Vilhon Wilds. The lands and ruins were once again submerged in 1486 DR by the Great Rain and Second Sundering.

## Inessa

Sister Inessa was an undead nurse who worked alongside Malus Thorm, a servant of the goddess Shar, of the House of Healing in the town of Reithwin throughout the 15 century DR.

History
Sister Inessa lived in the town of Reithwin in the Western Heartlands before its fall sometime before the . During that time, Inessa and other nurses performed healing and other medical procedures in Reithwin's House of Healing. The nurses entertained themselves by making wagers on what sorts of foreign objects they would extract after being lodged in their patients' backside orifice.

At some point during the town's fall into dark worship of Shar or after the Shadow-cursed Lands consumed Reithwin in the late 14 century DR, Sister Inessa perished and was changed by the dark Shadowfell magic. As of the late 15 century DR, Malus Thorm and his nurses operated out of the House of Healing in the Shadow-cursed lands. They spent their unlives "experimenting" on innocents that either wandered, or were brought against their will, into his hospital. Malus attempted to teach his Sisters the finer arts of what he called surgery but was ultimately unsuccessful. Sister Inessa's undead form was slain by the, her body unmoving in the House of Healing's operating theater.

## Infernal rapier

The infernal rapier was an enchanted weapon that harnessed the infernal magics of Avernus from the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The rapier possessed a +2 enchantment that made its physical attacks stronger and guided the wielder's arm toward the targets. Additionally, the blade's wielder damage output was as great as their spellcasting abilities. As such, the infernal rapier boosted the wielder's resistance to enemy spells. Once per day, the infernal rapier allowed the wielder to cast the planar ally spell, summoning a cambion.

History
The infernal rapier belonged to Mizora, a fiendish servant of Zariel who was captured by the cult of the Absolute in the . She had the weapon fashioned to represent the soul of her warlock, Wyll Ravengard, summarizing it in heated infernal steel. She offered the weapon as a reward for her warlock, were he to rescue Mizora from the mind flayer colony underneath the Moonrise Towers.

## Inn Plain Sight

The Inn Plain Sight was an inn in the late 15 century DR. Its proprietor was Alax Jadescales, a dragonborn.

Description
The Inn Plain Sight was an unassuming roadside establishment attached to a decaying tower. The inn was built not far from the mountain lair of the red dragon Cinderhowl. The inn itself had two floors – a barroom on the first with the kitchens where the proprietor kept a variety of dried chilies and magically preserved unrefrigerated chicken, and a backroom, and guest lodgings on the second.

History

Sometime in the late 15 century DR, an evil sorcerer called Ervan Soulfallen embarked on a quest to procure a red dragon egg. He magically disguised himself, imprisoned Alax Jadescales, and took on the alias of "Merry Rumwell," the inn's "new owner." Soon after, an adventuring band called the Hard Way passed through the inn, where the sorcerer hired the group to find and retrieve the egg in exchange for coin and hot wings. To no surprise, Ervan Soulfallen paid the adventurers with swift death instead of hot wings and stashed their bodies in the inn's basement. Even though the sorcerer reached his goal, he remained at the inn under the assumed guise, in fear of the dragon's wrath.

Ervan hoped to remain at the Inn Plain Sight until the egg hatched so the hatchling could be indoctrinated into servitude. However, that was not to be. Another group of adventurers – an orc rogue, a gnome fighter, an elf wizard, and a dwarvish cleric arrived and were sure to foil the sorcerer's schemes.

He attributed the revelation to his servant Indur, who tried to follow the ghosts, only to find they vanished through a wall; upon checking, the servant could find no illusion or secret door.

## Indrina Lamsensettle

Indrina Lamsensettle was a Neverwintian actress in the late 15 century DR. She belonged to the elite social circles of Neverwinter.

History
At some point, Indrina paid for genealogical research into the family history of Neverwinter's ruler, Dagult Neverember.

In the late 1490s DR, Indrina and other three nobles were kidnapped by a group of Vecna cultists and taken to Neverwinter's graveyard, Neverdeath. Indrina was certain that the mastermind behind her kidnapping was none other than Dagult Neverember. She believed he was planning to silence her to prevent her from divulging information that would prove Lord Neverember had no blood ties to Lord Nasher Alagondar. In truth, he was unconcerned with her findings.

## Incontinentia Figgin

Incontinentia Figgin was a drow from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Incontinentia was a drow woman with long white hair gathered in a ponytail. She wore a set of armor dyed green.

History
In the, a large group of duergar, giant spiders, and drow, was slaughtered in the home of a Bloomridge Park groundskeeper, Lavernica. The murder spree was performed by Orin the Red and her Murder Tribunal of Bhaal. Incontinentia's body was gutted and displayed in a grotesque display at the center of a small cave under the groundskeeper's home. Her body was turned into an effigy representing Sendai, a drow Bhaalspawn who died a century earlier at the hands of Abdel Adrian. The grisly display was embellished with the bodies of Lavernica, Broom Boy Tigrellpie, Josef the Ferret, Fluemartina B. Martelles, Abaforth Zelksix, Appleblossom Cathraelk, Damn 'Em All Holmwood, Zybil Hastenwald, and Cotre Silkwright.

## Ingrath

Ingrath was a guard at the House of the Unicorn in Nethrar, Galadorna, ing the mid-8 century DR.

Personality
Ingrath considered youthful dalliances to be something he'd rememorate about, despite being young.

Description
He was young.

Relationships
He was closely loyal to the realm's seneschal, Elminster Aumar.

History
Ingrath was serving as a guard at the House of the Unicorn, the royal home of Galadorna, in Nethrar, late in the,In the ebook version of Temptation, the Realmslore section declares the events of the first part to happen in around five years starting 759 DR. In the first chapter, Nuressa, the warrior, tells Elminster they're in early Mirtul. If it is assumed that the events happened in at most five and a half years, as more than five and a half years becomes around six years, then the last events in Part 1 must happen at most during Uktar of 764 DR. Given Feldrin's warning during the coronation that the Lords of Laothkund are already planning an attack, this timeline seems plausible. Further development may yet alter the result. when he and fellow guard Delver did some services for the seneschal. His last service involved personally burning scrolls, then mixing the ashes with a dust provided by the seneschal, and casting them into the moat. The seneschal then wiped away his memory of doing that and left him wandering, recalling youthful dalliances. The memory wipe was almost certainly because the seneschal sought to blunt the cruelties of the sorceress-queen of Galadorna, Dasumia, which strongly implied the scrolls were hers.

Later at the Goblet of Shadows, a local tavern, Ingrath and Daluth were propositioned by a disguised Dasumia into some ungentlemanlike evening activities She took them to a shrine of Bane hidden somewhere in the House of the Unicorn, where they were forced to participate in a deadly ritual. After being affected by some compulsion spell by Dasumia and whipping her until she was red and bloody, she noticed the seneschal, Elminster, hiding in the entrance. After issuing their challenges, Dasumia sent the mind-controlled guards against him. He used a spell to release them, but they were knocked out by the backlash of the unwinding magic.

Interrogation in Three Levels was a textbook used to teach Sharran initiates skills of interrogation, printed sometime in or before the 15 century DR.

The text book collected information on how to inflict three levels of stress during interrogations: emotional, physical, and lethal. ## Incineration collar

Incineration collars, or simply kill collars, were tools of slavery and subjugation used by the cultists of Bane in Baldur's Gate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Incineration collars were made as a series of sharp-edged purse crystals encased in a collar chassis of gold. The crystals glowed when activated and when they resonated with the corresponding Prinski's Motivators.

Powers
Each Motivator was connected to a number of incineration collars always located nearby; once activated, they could instantly kill all the collared persons. If the Black Gauntlet dropped the crystal device, it automatically activated with some delay, killing every shackled slave around it. If Prinski's Motivator was handled by someone unaware of its inner workings, it had a chance of detonating itself, along with the nearby incineration collars.

History
As of the, Motivators and incineration collars were used by the Black Gauntlets of Bane under the leadership of Lord Enver Gortash to "motivate" Gondian workers of the Steel Watch Foundry to produce steel watchers around the clock. Followers of Bane, the god of tyranny, insisted that enslaved Gondians remained aware of what they were forced to do and suffered from knowing what they'd done.

## Infernal armor animus

Infernal armor animuses, sometimes shortened to just infernal armors, were souls bound to suits of armor by devils through an evil ritual.

Description
An infernal armor animus might appear as general scrap or detritus until it was activated. However, even when lying on the floor the buckles and clasps remained fastened, and some of it might visibly move.

Behavior
Despite their similarity to constructs, infernal armors were relatively (if not greatly) intelligent.

Abilities

Infernal armor animuses empowered stronger devils near them with their aura of bloodlust, making their allies hit harder for each aura that enveloped them. Though weak, the destruction of their physical forms caused the soul within to burst free, only to be consumed by the nearest devil, healing them with the transference of essence.

Combat
Infernal armors were usually equipped with short swords and heavy shields, and hurled themselves into battle, flanking when possible. They were most effective when massed with more powerful devils and moved to stay near as many as possible to keep them in their aura.

Infernal armors may hold back initially, as being destroyed before others were harmed would be a waste of their abilities, or remained still until a certain subject was harmed, at which point they charged and attacked. As such, the best tactic when confronted with them was often to destroy them all before attacking other devils.

Society

The tortured spirits within infernal armors provided a cache of life energy for devils and military support to their overlords. It was possible to restrain souls within a magic circle before deactivating the circle, allowing the souls to turn nearby armor into infernal armor.

History
In the late years of the 15 century DR, the Ashmadai cells of Neverwinter were known to make use of infernal armor.

## Inete

Inete was an acolyte of Savras active in Port Nyanzaru in Chult during the late 15 century DR.

Possessions
She was decently wealthy (enough to fund an expedition into the Chultan jungle). She lived and kept her possessions at Port Nyanzaru's Temple of Savras.

Relationships
She was a member of Port Nyanzaru's Temple of Savras, and was given a great deal of leeway from the Temple's head, Grandfather Zitembe. She was quick to introduce new allies to her wise leader.

History
During the events of the death curse, Inete was plagued by troubling visions of red-robed wizards establishing a secret base in the Aldani Basin deep in the Chultan jungle. She spoke with Grandfather Zitembe about her visions, who was too busy to help her investigate but gave her permission to travel to the Aldani Basin herself. She thus sought out adventurers already planning to travel into the jungle in hopes of joining them.

## In the Mode of Masters

In the Mode of Masters was a book on the Astral Period in Faerûnean art, penned sometime before the late 15 century DR.

Contents
The book stated that Wilhelmina Oates was the creator of the Astral Period of art that followed the Classical Period and was in opposition to eachother. The Astral Period was not constrained by the color and material limitations of the true-to-life Classical Period and instead utilized porous borders between segments and included the use of various unconventional materials in painting, like blood and stone, hair sewn into the canvas, and chalk used to depict skin. Wilhelmina Oates was the person who created the very first distinct piece of art of the Astral Period, allegedly after returning to the Prime Material plane from a lifetime at the Astral Sea. She discovered that no time had passed on Prime since her departure and channeled her accumulated wisdom into a new art style. At first, Wilhelmina's art was maligned by the critics, but eventually, it was accepted and hailed a new era of creation in Faerûn.

History
In the, a copy of In the Mode of Masters belonged to the acclaimed painter Oskar Fevras, who kept a copy in his workshop atop his wife's Lower City manse.

## Innate talent

Innate talents were unique abilities that awakened at first contact with magic.

Overview
Anyone who was touched by magic for the first time had a small, 1 in a 100, chance of developing one such innate talent; that chance was doubled for those who were the sole recipients of that magic. Those who were the progeny of two people with innate talents of their own had a 1 in 25 chance of possessing such a power, with a 1 in 100 chance that their magic would manifest at the time of their birth; otherwise, it would awaken later in life at their first contact with magic at the normal rate. Such powers also awakened from being in intensely magical places, such as Thar.

Possible Powers
These innate talents varied in frequency and reliability, and many were deleterious to use. Many of them were much like known spells such as clairaudience, detect good or evil, detect snares and pits, detect thoughts, ironguard, levitate, stone tell, telekinesis, temporal stasis, time stop, tree shape, or water walk. Other talents did not resemble known spells, being instead a perfect awareness of true north or the ability to deflect all spells or mental attacks, to see infrared, to locate water, to gain visions of objects handled, or to sometimes predict another creature's actions perfectly. Other abilities were akin to those granted by the great elixir. In rare cases, two talents coexisted, and every day one of them would be manifest. Talents were also known to change sometimes, or even fade away entirely after a few years. Other known powers were a perfect imitation of others' speech, down to the user's ability to mimic the target's vocabulary beyond what they'd ever heard them speak, and other audible mannerisms.

According to Volothamp Geddarm in Volo's Guide to All Things Magical edited by Elminster, people who displayed such powers were always incapable of learning magic.

Society
Knowledge of innate talents was not shared freely by wizards, who were mostly not interested in testing the idle curious for powers developed upon contact with magic.

History
Such powers had been known since ancient times. During the late 14 century DR, the sage Eltrivyn of Candlekeep was studying such folk and offered them sanctuary in exchange for an opportunity to study them.

Rumors and Legends
Tales about such powers often were about people who struck back at overbearing wizards with newfound powers in moments of great oppression. Many who developed such powers feared they may have been cursed, and were in turn feared as possible "fiendkin".

Appendix
See Also
 Wild talents, much alike but psionic in nature.

Ingval Muth was an elder resident of Eveningstar in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Ingval was an older man with a bald head and short white goatee. He wore a long black robe.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Ingval sought the help of passing adventurers (from Eberron) in regards to the missing War Wizard Barthand, who had gone and investigated the cave system below the ruins of a burnt-out house on the edge of Eveningstar in pursuit of the strange dreams and voices that had been haunting the people of Eveningstar. Though a capable War Wizard, Barthand did not return. When the adventurers showed up, Ingval explained the situation in depth and asked that they investigate the caves and rescue the wizard. ## Inku Neza

Inku Neza was a barbarian berserker member of the Dead Eyes, a criminal group from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate that named itself for the grim trophies they collected from their enemies, in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Inku was a dragonborn woman with flaming red eyes, brown scales, and long horns that curved inward. She wore a set of splint mail armor and was armed with two handaxes.

In the, the Dead Eyes set out to perform a job that would end up setting the gang against the Guild and its leader, Ninefingers. The Guild did not take competition lightly and sent thugs to take care of the Dead Eyes. The band survived and relocated to a new lair. Unbeknownst to Aideen Ni Chuirc, the gang's leader, one of her men was a traitor, informing the Guild of the lair and the hidden stash's location. The traitor bard instructed Ninefingers's men to strike at the hideout quickly and hard. ## Instant door seed

Instant door seeds were mundane seeds, enchanted to conjure forth an escape route to their owner.

Powers
Each seed contained a single charge of the dimension door spell. To activate an instant door seed, the owner had to throw a seed against a hard surface. When activated, a seed opened a dimension door, invisible to all but the seed's owner and one other creature chosen by them. The two-way gate could transport the seed's owner and one creature of their choice away from their current whereabouts. Usually, instant door seeds were sold in padded bags from three to thirty seeds per bag.

History
Instant door seeds were one of the many magical creations of Elminster Aumar, the Sage of Shadowdale, when he was a mere youth, a fledgling mage. He was captured by a bandit band and imprisoned in a castle's dungeon. He escaped thanks to coming up with the idea of enchanting a handful of seeds with the dimension door spell earlier.

Appendix
Behind the Scenes
Instant door seed was a magic item created by Anthony Marzotto from Toronto, Ontario for the Disposable Magical Item Contest held by slade. Anthony's creation was one of the contest's runner-ups.

Background

It is copied almost verbatim from that product, going so far as to reuse the map for the first level. The monsters, however, are changed for the Realms. Among those changes, Tinker the gnome is accosted by bugbears instead of kobolds, there is a ju-ju zombie among the skeletons, carrion crawlers instead of fire beetles, a giant trapdoor spider replaces four giant rats, five orcs rather than three, giant snakes instead of regular. There are ten friendly gnomes instead of five who offer an enchanted sword instead of money, giant bats instead of an ooze, gnolls instead of goblins with a flame tongue instead of a sword of wounding, and Kordak is two levels higher, boasting two zombies instead of one. Furthermore, the second level featured a "wet room" with lizard men instead of a "paper room" with wasps, and a gelatinous cube instead of ghouls for its burnt room.

## Insurance

Insurance, also known as surety, was a means of protecting from financial loss in which, in exchange for a fee, one party agreed to compensate another in the event of a contingent loss, damage, or injury. This service could be found in the cities of Baldur's Gate, Ravens Bluff, and Waterdeep, as well as to a lesser extent all over the Heartlands, along the trade routes of the Sword Coast, and parts of Wildspace. The entity who provided insurance was called either an insurer or underwriter, while the one buying it was referred to as the policyholder.

Description
When buying insurance, the person or entity receives a written contract, presided over at a temple, called a "performance bond" or "insurance policy". This transaction was most often presided over within a Waukeenar temple. These contracts detailed the conditions and circumstances under which an insurer had to compensate the insured.

In Faerûn the insurers of these policies were moneylenders, some guilds, and increasingly over time governments and rulers. In some instances there existed businesses centered around this service, known as insurance companies. Government-issued performance bonds were provided to adventurers, costers, or guilds willing to undertake a risky task on behalf of the insuring ruler.

There existed a wide variety of insurance types, each devoted to providing protection against the loss or injury of a specific thing or in a specific manner. These ranged from particular items of property, vehicles like ships and spelljammers, the cargo on a ship, buildings, limbs, or even the death of a specific person. Fire bonds were surety for houses that obligated a guarantor to either put out a house fire or, more often, compensate for any lost goods. Tumbledown surety, also known as "windstorm surety", compelled an insurer in the cases of a building collapsing to pay for the construction of a new one and the compensation of all property.  Theft insurance could protect a sailing ship from suffering a financial loss from piracy or dockside pilferage. And spoilage insurance could protect a ship from the financial loss of perishables going bad due to unforeseeable time delays, like dockworkers striking, bureaucratic port delays, or long periods without wind. However, performance bonds could not cover every unforeseeable form of loss, and it was up to buyers to be savvy of what coverage they might need.

For insurers, there was sometimes a risk of policyholders trying to defraud them, such as burning down their own insured building. When insurers have reason to suspect foul play was at work, they would hire investigators to prove the validity of their claims. A plaintiff might even have to submit themselves to magical scrutiny in order to prove their innocence.

Notable Insurers
 Cheesemore, a Lathanderite priest in Ravens Bluff, under the permission of the Church of Lathander and Chief Prelate Relarn Dayspring, began offering in 1370 DR a "holy innovation" that he dubbed "Life Insurance policies". In return for a stiff fee of 100,000 gold pieces, no questions asked, the Halls of Morning Light would do whatever was necessary into order to restore the policyholder back to life in the case of an untimely demise. A group of people (such as an adventuring company) could all contribute to a policy, though only the first to die would benefit from it. Furthermore, the body of the deceased had to be brought to the Halls – their contracts had no clause obligating the church to retrieve the body. Many Ravenians were skeptical of the policies, as Cheesemore had a prior criminal history, though none of the members of the city's Clerical Circle dared to challenge Relarn's pet project.
 The Dock, a spelljamming port in the Utter East that was controlled by mercane, offered all who docked there the option of docking insurance at a rate of 1 silver piece per every ten tons of their spelljammer. They guaranteed a replacement of any materials that stolen, destroyed, or damaged while a craft was moored. However, they would not compensate for any losses caused by acts of war, acts of gods, curses, or the baneful effects of artifacts."Tonnage" in Spelljammer products typically referred to the volume occupied by a ship in spatial tons, different from the typical tonnage of seafaring ships. One spatial ton was equivalent to a volume of 100 cubic yards (76 cubic meters).
 Fishermen and Merchants' Collective of Dragonmere, an insurance coster that presumably operated in the lands around the Dragonmere.
 Frozenfar Expeditions, an adventuring shop in Caer-Konig, sold search-and-rescue insurance policies to explorers of the Frozenfar region. Explorers would put up postings on the shop's noticeboard, detailing their expedition plan and date of return. If they did not return by that time, Atenas Swift would use their deposit to hire a group of adventurers to attempt a rescue.
 The Safe Harbor Marine Insurance Company, a Ravenian business that specialized in providing marine insurance, that being protection for ships and their cargo, as well as theft and spoilage insurance. Its marine insurance specifically offered compensation in the case of a shipwreck or damage from fire or salt water. Their fees varied depending upon a ship's reputation and whether or not its owner or captain was a regular client. Safe Harbor later expanded to offering property insurance for valuable pieces of art, jewelry and furniture. Not long after, experiencing a number of insurance frauds, the business further expanded to employing its own roster of investigators to inspect suspicious losses of property.
 Simon Weems, an insurance salesman from Earth who briefly traveled to Toril.

## Influenza

Influenza, commonly called the flu and also known as grippe, was a notable non-magical disease that was present on Toril.

Acquisition
Influenza was infectious, and an influenza plague could sweep through a realm, seeming to travel at a rate of  per day. Travelers were advised that they could catch the flu when traveling in cold and/or heavily rainy weather.It is a common misconception that the flu arises from cold weather or a chill. This information is included for faithfulness to the source text and setting. It could also be caught passively while in populated areas, especially by those in poor health. Unlike some diseases, a prior case of influenza did not prevent reinfection.

Effects
The flu left infected individuals bedridden and could result in death.

About 1 in 5 who became infected in a bad influenza outbreak might die, with the survivors being completely incapacitated or weakened for about a tenday and a half. Influenza plagues could wipe out as much as 10% of a population, with humans being notably susceptible. Even so, it was said generally to be one of the least dangerous infectious diseases.

Treatment
It was said that virulent plagues of influenza were resistant to magical cures, such as spells or potions.

## Infinity train

The infinity train was a dangerous magical weapon powered by soul larvae and created by Yrkhetep to defeat Anthraxus.

Description
The infinity train was made from five levitating carts, crammed full of modified soul larvae. The larvae appeared as spirits, and were constantly producing the sounds of ghostly wailing. The carts shone brightly, were intangible, and moved at an unlimited speed.

The infinity train ran on a track made of an undulating glowing white tube  in diameter. Part of the track ran along the ground, and parts were raised off the ground, reaching as high as . The track began and ended in conduit-type portals that connected to other parts of the lower planes. These portals appeared to be filled with thick fog and drew objects and creatures within  into them.

It was powered by soul larvae that were stored in a nearby cylindrical tank, measuring  tall and just under  in diameter. This immense component was made from iron and had no distinguishing characteristics. It was positioned by a pool of lava.

The infinity train was resistant to all magic and evaded all physical attacks. The track, however, was vulnerable and able to be cut. If the Train ran into two parts of destroyed track, it would derail and instantly be destroyed.

Powers
The infinity train was a powerful weapon that fired bursts of bright white specks of energy.

History
The arcanaloth Yrkhetep planned to usurp his ruler the oinoloth Anthraxus. To this end, Yrkhetep created the Infinity Train to lead an army and attack Khin-Oin, where Anthraxus resided. He secretly caused wars in the Vilhon Reach to collect evil souls that powered his Train. As part of these wars, he schemed to obtain the Hand of Tyr and use it as a power source for the Infinity Train. Yrkhetep kept the infinity train a secret, as it was to be his secret weapon against Anthraxus.

Meanwhile, while the infinity train was not yet completed, Anthraxus became aware of Yrkhetep's schemes and sought to observe the weapon himself. He inserted visions into the mind of Inata Eller, manipulating her into gathering armies together and attacking Yrkhetep for him. Following Inata's visions, a council was formed: representatives from Timindar, Ormath, Gildenglade, and Hlath. The mayor of Amah, Pachipol, summoned adventurers and the Iron Legion to the woods south of Arrabar.

Inata led this heroic force through a portal into Oinos, where they attempted to destroy the still unfinished Train in an battle against Yrkhetep, and his army of  100 lava mephits, skeletons, zombies, hordlings and diakka. During the battle for the infinity train, Yrkhetep was also served by Multon Weny, Drado Weny and the Master of the Realm, a quasit. The battle was watched by Anthraxus and three ultroloths, who planned to destroy the infinity train's storage tank if the Iron Legion overwhelmed Yrkhetep.

Before the battle, Yrkhetep's plans involved building more storage tanks, filled with millions of soul larvae, to increase the power of the Train.

## Inn of the Downstream Run

The Inn of the Downstream Run was an inn and tavern located in the Northbank of Silverymoon, near the River Rauvin and the Moonbridge, in the mid–14 century DR.

Location
The inn stood on the southern end of Brightbuckler Street, just one door away from the riverbank.The game's map of Silverymoon is a very rough approximation of a small central portion of the city. The location of the Inn of the Downstream Run is approximately placed based on the street layout and number of doors in relation to the Moonbridge, the River Rauvin, and the general direction of the Market of Silverymoon.

Interior
If a visitor were to mention one thing of note about the Inn of the Downstream Run, they would surely mention the driftglobes that floated above the common and dining rooms, shedding their lights at the dinners. The magic globes changed colors when commanded by their mage creator or the inn's staff. However, the Downstream's driftglobes also radiated warmth and emitted certain scents that were controlled to enhance the guests' experiences.

Services
The Inn of the Downstream Run offered its guests food, drink, and lodgings.

History
In the, the Inn of the Downstream Run offered special rates to the guests visiting the city and attending the Festival of Mielikki, held annually in Mielikki's Glade.

## Inger Ingersdottir

Inger Ingersdottir was a dwarf who served as the personal assistant of Hal Stoutheart during his Tethyamar expedition in the 1480s DR.

Description
Inger had light skin and long flowing brown hair. She wore a very fine outfit (setting her apart from the miners and guards of the expedition) and several pieces of jewelry.

Personality

Inger was a very capable woman, but was utterly dazzled by Hal's (or rather, Graz'zt's) personality and joined the general mania around the jewels of ardor. She became somewhat obsessed with his greatness, and this led her to overlook the inconsistencies in his story and the strange events around the expedition.

Activities
Inger practically ran the Tethyamar mining outpost for Hal, handling the recruitment, logistics, and excavations.

History

Inger managed Hal's affairs as the expedition set up in the Tethyamar mining outpost and began mining the jewels of ardor. She was kept extremely busy throughout this, as the dwarves fell prey to natural hazards and monsters, the undead, the House Argith drow, and their own creeping madness. This came to a head when her master suddenly vanished into the Eastern Reaches and did not return. She sent some useful hirelings to find Hal, and after some confusing and horrifying trials (including the village of Faraway) they discovered that Hal had died in Blue Stone Cavern years ago, and the leader of the expedition was in fact the demon lord Graz'zt.

Inger was quite rocked to learn that she had been the personal assistant of Graz'zt, and questioned everything she had believed in life. Despite this, she kept positive attitude and looked forward to fixing things.

## Inder Dunseltree

Inder Dunseltree was a merchant of Scornubel during the 14 century DR. He was impersonated by drow during the mid-to-late part of that century.This article refers to both him and the drow who impersonated him, as no name is given for the latter.

Description
The drow impersonating him took on his appearance, which included a florid face.

Possessions
He owned a lavish home.

Activities
Inder was a merchant, the owner of Softer Tapestries.

Relationships
He was married to Namra Dunseltree, and employed a number of servants, including a girl named Isryl. His wife was replaced in turn by the drow Anlaervrith Mrantarr, whom his own replacement privately treated with intense disdain. Curiously, they had an arrangement regarding each other's flirtations with other people, as though they had some kind of emotional or legal attachment which did not show otherwise.

History
The owner of Softer Tapestries, Inder Dunseltree was replaced by a drow infiltrator at some point on or before, and sent off to Chult. On that year, the drow infiltrator participated in a mission to get Halonder Eldeglut replaced by another drow. Unknown to him, his partner Anlaervrith Mrantarr had in turn been replaced by a different drow, Qilué Veladorn, one of the Seven Sisters.

During the infiltration, he and his partner (who had in turn been replaced without his knowledge) took care of distracting Halonder and his wife Iyrevven Eldeglut so that their wine could be poisoned. They were successful at this task before the one replacing his partner blew her cover.

## Inn of the Weary Pilgrim

The Inn of the Weary Pilgrim was a mountainside inn somewhere in Faerûn, surrounded by tall trees, mountains, an asperii herd, and was visited by nobles (and presumably pilgrims) in the 14 century DR.

History
Sometime in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the inn's garden's mint plants were ravaged, its fishing pond was emptied of its fish, mistletoe was plucked, and a visiting nobleman's pet hawk went missing. The panicked innkeeper hired a band of adventurers to uncover the culprits. It was revealed that a nearby heard of asperii might've been to blame; local hunters were planning to capture the young "wind steeds" for sale. Eventually, the heroes found out that the culprits were Silverhoof and Sparkle, young twin steeds, who had captured by a nearby nest of griffons to feed to their young. The adventurers met the missing twins' parents and allied with them against the hunters and the griffons, eventually saving the youths. The Inn of the Weary Pilgrim, however, had to come to terms with its losses.

## Intrusive taste link

Intrusive taste link was a rare psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline. Developed by illithids, it allowed a manifester to create a sensory bond between them and another creature, making the target taste whatever the psionic user tasted.

Effects

This power operated similarly to the more common taste link, though linked its manifester's taste buds to the target instead of the other way around and had a limited range of .

Significance
This power was usually used between illithids themselves as part of "performance eating", a psionic pastime in illithid culture wherein an audience sensory bonded to the performer through intrusive taste link shared in their artistic consumption of a thrall's brain. But some, often those of the Abysmal Creed, were found to use the power on their human and humanoid rivals when gorging upon the brain of one of their compatriots, a traumatizing act that shocked and induced nausea on the victims. And others, taken to a twisted sense of humor, would set up intrusive taste links with the humanoids they were eating the brain of.

Users
This power was not uncommon among moderately powerful illithids,While page 86 of The Illithiad lists intrusive taste link as one of the psionic disciplines for the "Standard Illithid", page 28 stated that "...it is rare for a common illithid to know even one unique psionic power" and that "Common illithids have [only] a 10% to roll once on the [unique psionic power] table..." as well as ulitharids and elder brains. The psionic masters of the race entrusted guardianship over knowledge of intrusive taste link to elder brains, who then passed it down to future generations.

Known/Notable Users
 Lugribossk, a proxy of the illithid god Ilsensine.The stat-block for Lugribossk says only "taste link", but includes an asterisk next to the term, indicating that it's referring to the intrusive taste link introduced in the same book.
 The members of the Tamer Capture Squad, a Tamer Creed group in the illithid city of Nar'Vheen that captured creatures of other races, typically knew this power.
 Yahol, one of the leaders of Nar'Vheen.

History
In the late 14 century DR, a group of Ravenian adventurers were unknowingly manipulated by the illithids of Nar'Vheen into heading to their city, being either taken as prisoners or brought as honored guests for helping to fight against a drow assault on Nar'Vheen, in order to forge a mutual alliance treaty with the Underdark community of Ravens Deep. After a successful negotiation, the Elder Concord member Yagol, offered the adventurers the chance to experience their unusual dietary habits through the use of intrusive taste link, reasoning that "It is only fair that you know how we eat, for we would not want to enter into this agreement with misconceptions."

Appendix
See Also
 Intrusive sense link, a psionic power that could forge a sensory link with unwilling creatures.

## Invigorating flame

Invigorating flame was a unique and strangely specific conjuration spell that empowered defeated grease elementals, created by Aelis Siryasius the mad made of the Baldurian sewers sometime in the late 15 century DR.

Effects
This spell could be cast upon the corpses of defeated grease elementals, such as grease mephits and grease elementals, within 60 ft. and visible to the caster. When the spell was cast, it ignited grease-filled corpses with revitalising fires, returning them to life as more powerful flame elemental creatures. Grease mephit were resurrected as magma mephits and grease elementals as lava elementals. The more powerful the caster was, the more powerful the elementals became.

Description
Inter Mierionydd had a variety of colorful political figures that vied for control of the nation and of the Whamite Isles at large. The people of Inter Mierionydd were also represented by the clans of Nordor and Mier.

Inter Mierionydd shared land borders with Graubünden, Bolgor, and the Cold Desert. The border with the Cold Desert was marked by a mountain range. Inter Mierionydd was also the location of the well-defended Castle on the Mount

Among the nations of the Whamites, Inter Mierionydd was significant for the religious hierarchy that led it.

## Invidia

Invidia was one of the Domains of Dread. It was a land of largely unspoiled wilderness and rivers.

Description
The domain was mostly wilderness. Invidia's largest city, Karina, was home to only two thousand people, and was surrounded by vineyards. The people were for some reason especially prone to crimes of passion.

## Index (scroll)

An index was a magical scroll created for the express purpose of holding a spell used in the creation of a magical item.

Description
A wide variety of indexes existed, each able to hold different amounts of magical power and complexity according to the materials and expertise used in their creation. The writing surface could be paper, parchment, or a similar material, and the ink was usually mixed with powdered metal. This ink was used to draw the arcane diagrams and ciphers that contained the spell to be used.

Known indexes included:
 Simple index, made with cheap paper and ink, able to hold only the weakest of magic.
 Cypren index, made with copper-based ink.
 Argent index, made with silver-based ink.
 Amber index, made with electrum-based ink.
 Aureus index, made with gold-based ink.
 Mithrus index, made with mithral-based ink.

## Infiltrator

An infiltrator was an elite scout within elvish society, a master of disguise and operating covertly in both urban and sylvan environments alike. They had a reputation for being able to acquire reliable intel quickly and quietly, even delighting in the unearthing of secrets.

Culture
A substantial number of infiltrators could be found across Faerûn, typically living lives as guides, mercenaries, or spies. In some rare cases, they were even professional thieves. But on Evermeet, infiltrators played an integral part in the island nation's defenses.

Infiltrators were not limited to any one subrace of elves, but were most commonly found amongst the likes of drow, high elves, and sylvan elves. The practice could be taken up by the likes of mages, warriors, and rogues.

Due to the extensive periods they spent in the role of others, infiltrators over time risked losing their sense of identity and becoming less sure of themselves than other elves. Their race's special meditative state, the reverie, proved inadequate in preventing this slippage. As a consequence of this bruise to their sense of identity, infiltrators lost some of racial resistance to charm-related and sleep spells.

Possessions
Infiltrators varied considerably in the types of equipment they carried, but they were always guaranteed to have a quality disguise kit on their person. With their mastery of disguise, an infiltrator could pass themselves off as a short human or tall dwarf. They also lacked any form of distinguishing mark in their appearance, thus if an elf wanted to find an infiltrator they had to rely upon word of mouth.

Notable Infiltrators
 Rennyn Aelorothi, an elite spy of Queen Amlaruil Moonflower of Evermeet.
